340	/-- Half-open byte range `[base, base+len)` in `toNat` (no wrap — all our
341	    addresses sit far below 2⁶⁴). -/
342	def memRange (a base : Word) (len : Nat) : Prop :=
343	  base.toNat ≤ a.toNat ∧ a.toNat < base.toNat + len
344	def memRangeB (a base : Word) (len : Nat) : Bool :=
345	  base.toNat ≤ a.toNat && a.toNat < base.toNat + len
346	
347	/-- A machine-private hole: the `[ra][slots]` area an activation adds BELOW its
348	    user frame — `(base = that activation's sp, len = userOff)`. Listed per live
349	    activation by the `CallChain` ghost (Phase 5). -/
350	abbrev Hole := Word × Nat
351	
352	/-- Total loadable blob length (code + zero-pad + data segment). -/
353	def Layout.blobLen (L : Layout) : Nat := L.segStart + (dataSegment L.data).length
354	
355	/-- `MachPriv L holes a` — `a` is machine-private: inside the code+data blob, or
356	    inside some live activation's `[ra][slots]` hole. The IL never writes these;
357	    the footprint side condition forbids IL stores here. -/
358	def MachPriv (L : Layout) (holes : List Hole) (a : Word) : Prop :=
359	  memRange a L.codeBase L.blobLen ∨ ∃ h ∈ holes, memRange a h.1 h.2
360	def machPrivB (L : Layout) (holes : List Hole) (a : Word) : Bool :=
361	  memRangeB a L.codeBase L.blobLen || holes.any (fun h => memRangeB a h.1 h.2)
362

523	    sides. We capture this with `parkFold` — literally `frameEnter`'s fold — and
524	    show the machine memory tracks it pointwise on slots, off a shifted base. -/
525	
526	/-- The IL's `withParams` left fold, factored out (matches `frameEnter` verbatim):
527	    fold the `(param, argVal)` pairs into a register file, last write winning. -/
528	def parkFold (base : Nat → Word) (pairs : List (Nat × Word)) : Nat → Word :=
529	  pairs.foldl (fun rf pv => fun r => if r = pv.1 then pv.2 else rf r) base
530	
531	@[simp] theorem parkFold_nil (base : Nat → Word) : parkFold base [] = base := rfl
532	
533	theorem parkFold_cons (base : Nat → Word) (p : Nat × Word) (ps : List (Nat × Word)) :
534	    parkFold base (p :: ps) = parkFold (fun r => if r = p.1 then p.2 else base r) ps := rfl
535	
536	/-- `parkFold b ps target` depends on `b` only through `b target`: two bases that
537	    agree at `target` give the same result (either `target` is a fold key — then
538	    the answer is a paired value — or it isn't, and the answer is `b target`). -/
539	theorem parkFold_base_congr (target : Nat) :
540	    ∀ (ps : List (Nat × Word)) (b1 b2 : Nat → Word), b1 target = b2 target →
541	      parkFold b1 ps target = parkFold b2 ps target
542	  | [], _, _, h => h
543	  | p :: ps, b1, b2, h => by
544	      rw [parkFold_cons, parkFold_cons]
545	      apply parkFold_base_congr target ps
546	      by_cases hp : target = p.1
547	      · rw [if_pos hp, if_pos hp]
548	      · rw [if_neg hp, if_neg hp]; exact h
549	
550	/-- If `target` is not among the fold keys, `parkFold` just reads the base there. -/
551	theorem parkFold_not_mem (target : Nat) :
552	    ∀ (ps : List (Nat × Word)) (b : Nat → Word), target ∉ ps.map Prod.fst →
553	      parkFold b ps target = b target
554	  | [], _, _ => rfl
555	  | p :: ps, b, h => by
556	      simp only [List.map_cons, List.mem_cons, not_or] at h
557	      rw [parkFold_cons, parkFold_not_mem target ps _ h.2]
558	      simp only [if_neg h.1]
559	
560	/-- If `target` IS a fold key, the last write to it wins and the base is
561	    irrelevant: `parkFold` is independent of the base at that target. -/
562	theorem parkFold_mem_indep (target : Nat) :
563	    ∀ (ps : List (Nat × Word)) (b1 b2 : Nat → Word), target ∈ ps.map Prod.fst →
564	      parkFold b1 ps target = parkFold b2 ps target
565	  | [], _, _, h => by simp at h
566	  | p :: ps, b1, b2, h => by
567	      rw [parkFold_cons, parkFold_cons]
568	      simp only [List.map_cons, List.mem_cons] at h

301	/-- Every label position fits the 21-bit `jal`/branch offset window (from byte 0,
302	    so target − here ∈ (−2²⁰, 2²⁰) whenever both ends are `< 2²⁰`). -/
303	def LabelsOk (brkPos contPos : List Nat) (epiPos : Nat) : Prop :=
304	  (∀ p ∈ brkPos, p < 2 ^ 20) ∧ (∀ p ∈ contPos, p < 2 ^ 20) ∧ epiPos < 2 ^ 20
305	
306	/-- Every `ife`'s else-skip branch fits the 13-bit branch window (`8 + 4·csize e`
307	    = the compiler's `resolveOne` guard `δ ≤ 4094`). `while`'s branch is a fixed
308	    `+8`, always fine. A structural side condition, discharged per-program by the
309	    real layout (branches that don't fit make `Compile` return `none`). -/
310	def BranchOk : PStmt → Prop
311	  | .seq a b          => BranchOk a ∧ BranchOk b
312	  | .block body       => BranchOk body
313	  | .ife _ _ _ t e    => 8 + 4 * csize e < 2 ^ 12 ∧ BranchOk t ∧ BranchOk e
314	  | .while _ _ _ body => BranchOk body
315	  | _                 => True
316	
317	/-- A conditional branch `condInstr c T0 T1 δ` whose registers hold `x`, `y`: when
318	    `evalCond c x y` holds the branch is taken (`pc += δ`), matching the compiler's
319	    positive-form encoding (`.ge`/`.geu` take the *else* arm of `bge`/`bgeu`). -/
320	theorem cond_taken (m : State) (c : Cond) (δ : Int) (x y : Word)
321	    (hd : decode (fetch32 m) = condInstr c T0 T1 δ)
322	    (hx : m.rget T0 = x) (hy : m.rget T1 = y) (hev : evalCond c x y = true) :
323	    step m = m.setPc (m.pc + (BitVec.ofInt 13 δ).signExtend 64) := by
324	  cases c <;> simp only [condInstr] at hd
325	  · have h : x = y := by simpa [evalCond] using hev
326	    rw [step_beq m T0 T1 _ hd, hx, hy, if_pos h]
327	  · have h : x.slt y = true := by simpa [evalCond] using hev
328	    rw [step_blt m T0 T1 _ hd, hx, hy, if_pos h]
329	  · have h : x.slt y = false := by simpa [evalCond] using hev
330	    rw [step_bge m T0 T1 _ hd, hx, hy, if_neg (by simp [h])]
331	  · have h : x.ult y = false := by simpa [evalCond] using hev
332	    rw [step_bgeu m T0 T1 _ hd, hx, hy, if_neg (by simp [h])]
333	
334	/-- …and when `evalCond c x y` fails the branch falls through (`pc += 4`). -/

## What landed this session

**Closed the last statement-level `sorry`.** The `call` case assembly (segs 3–6 on top of the committed segs 1–2) now compiles with no `sorry`, and:

```
#print axioms lower_sim_cf = [propext, Quot.sound]
```

No `sorryAx`, no `Classical.choice` — the W8 target.

**The hard part** — reaching axiom-cleanliness — turned out to require clearing **pre-existing** `Classical.choice` taint that the earlier zero-init rework (`ec60b1a`/`53948c9`) had introduced. I bisected it with `sorry`-substitution + `#print axioms` down to three culprits:

1. `run_zeroFrame` — an `omega` handed a *negated* `memRange` conjunction `¬(P ∧ Q)` (bullet 5's positive `ha` was fine; bullet 6's negated `ha` pulled Classical). Fixed via a new constructive `not_memRange` helper.
2. `prologue_sim` — a `by_cases hfr : memRange …`, and `memRange` carries no `Decidable` instance so `by_cases` fell back to `Classical`. Fixed with a new constructive `memRange_or_not`.
3. My own `hmemF` — same negated-conjunction-into-`omega` pattern; rerouted through `not_memRange`.

The two lessons (already in memory, now reinforced in the docs): **never hand `omega` a `¬(P ∧ Q)`**, and **never `by_cases` an instance-less `Prop`**.

**Also:** fixed a latent over-strong `∀ s` no-wrap hypothesis in `run_retStoresFrom` (threaded per-state instead); added `hstackLo : stackLo = L.stackLo` (needed only for the c4 callee-hole/free-stack reconciliation, true by construction); added reusable `State_loadWord_congr8`/`loadWord_congr_range`; and trimmed `maxHeartbeats` from the conservative 1.6M down to 400k (verified sufficient).

**Verification:** full `lake build` green (60 jobs); `check_docs.py` OK (43 files).
